<p><b>Archibald Philip Primrose, 5th Earl of Rosebery, 1st Earl of Midlothian</b>, <span class="noexcerpt nowraplinks" style="font-size:100%; font-weight:normal;"><a href="/wiki/Knight_of_the_Order_of_the_Garter" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight of the Order of the Garter">KG</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Knight_Companion_of_the_Order_of_the_Thistle" class="mw-redirect" title="Knight Companion of the Order of the Thistle">KT</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">PC</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Fellow_of_the_Royal_Society" title="Fellow of the Royal Society">FRS</a><span style="font-size:14px;">&#44;</span>&#32;<a href="/wiki/Fellow_of_the_British_Academy" title="Fellow of the British Academy">FBA</a></span> (7 May 1847 – 21 May 1929) was a British <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Party_(UK)" title="Liberal Party (UK)">Liberal Party</a> politician who served as <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">Prime Minister of the United Kingdom</a> from March 1894 to June 1895. Between the death of his father, in 1851, and the death of his grandfather, the <a href="/wiki/Archibald_Primrose,_4th_Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Archibald Primrose, 4th Earl of Rosebery">4th Earl of Rosebery</a>, in 1868, he was known by the <a href="/wiki/Courtesy_titles_in_the_United_Kingdom" title="Courtesy titles in the United Kingdom">courtesy title</a> of <b>Lord Dalmeny</b>.
</p><p>Rosebery first came to national attention in 1879 by sponsoring the successful <a href="/wiki/Midlothian_campaign" title="Midlothian campaign">Midlothian campaign</a> of <a href="/wiki/William_Ewart_Gladstone" title="William Ewart Gladstone">William Ewart Gladstone</a>. He briefly was in charge of Scottish affairs. His most successful performance in office came as chairman of the <a href="/wiki/London_County_Council" title="London County Council">London County Council</a> in 1889. He entered the cabinet in 1885 and served twice as foreign minister, paying special attention to French and German affairs. He succeeded Gladstone as prime minister and leader of the Liberal Party in 1894; the Liberals lost the <a href="/wiki/1895_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1895 United Kingdom general election">1895 election</a>. He resigned the party leadership in 1896 and never again held political office.
</p><p>Rosebery was widely known as a brilliant <a href="/wiki/Orator" title="Orator">orator</a>, an outstanding sportsman and marksman, a writer and historian, connoisseur and collector. All of these activities attracted him more than politics, which grew boring and unattractive. Furthermore, he drifted to the right of the Liberal party and became a bitter critic of its policies. <a href="/wiki/Winston_Churchill" title="Winston Churchill">Winston Churchill</a>, observing that he never adapted to democratic electoral competition, quipped: "He would not stoop; he did not conquer."<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery was a <a href="/wiki/Liberal_Imperialist" class="mw-redirect" title="Liberal Imperialist">Liberal Imperialist</a> who favoured strong national defence and imperialism abroad and social reform at home, while being solidly <a href="/wiki/Anti-socialist" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-socialist">anti-socialist</a>. Historians judge him a failure as foreign minister<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> and as prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Origins_and_early_life">Origins and early life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Origins and early life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Archibald Philip Primrose was born on 7 May 1847 in his parents' house in <a href="/wiki/Charles_Street,_Mayfair" title="Charles Street, Mayfair">Charles Street, Mayfair</a>, London.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> His father was <a href="/wiki/Archibald_Primrose,_Lord_Dalmeny" title="Archibald Primrose, Lord Dalmeny">Archibald Primrose, Lord Dalmeny</a> (1809–1851), son and <a href="/wiki/Heir_apparent" title="Heir apparent">heir apparent</a> to <a href="/wiki/Archibald_Primrose,_4th_Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Archibald Primrose, 4th Earl of Rosebery">Archibald Primrose, 4th Earl of Rosebery</a> (1783–1868), whom he predeceased. Lord Dalmeny was a courtesy title used by the Earl's eldest son and heir apparent, during the Earl's lifetime, and was one of the Earl's lesser <a href="/wiki/Peerage_of_Scotland" title="Peerage of Scotland">Scottish titles</a>. Lord Dalmeny (died 1851) was MP for <a href="/wiki/Stirling_(UK_Parliament_constituency)" title="Stirling (UK Parliament constituency)">Stirling</a> from 1832 to 1847 and served as <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Admiralty" title="First Lord of the Admiralty">First Lord of the Admiralty</a> under <a href="/wiki/William_Lamb,_2nd_Viscount_Melbourne" title="William Lamb, 2nd Viscount Melbourne">Lord Melbourne</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery's mother was Lady <a href="/wiki/Catherine_Stanhope_(Duchess_of_Cleveland)" class="mw-redirect" title="Catherine Stanhope (Duchess of Cleveland)">(Catherine Lucy) Wilhelmina Stanhope</a> (1819–1901), a historian who later wrote under her second married name "the Duchess of Cleveland", a daughter of <a href="/wiki/Philip_Stanhope,_4th_Earl_Stanhope" class="mw-redirect" title="Philip Stanhope, 4th Earl Stanhope">Philip Stanhope, 4th Earl Stanhope</a>. Lord Dalmeny died on 23 January 1851, having predeceased his father, when the <a href="/wiki/Courtesy_title" title="Courtesy title">courtesy title</a> passed to his son, the future Rosebery, as the new heir to the earldom.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> In 1854 his mother remarried to Lord Harry Vane (later after 1864 known as <a href="/wiki/Harry_Powlett,_4th_Duke_of_Cleveland" title="Harry Powlett, 4th Duke of Cleveland">Harry Powlett, 4th Duke of Cleveland</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The relationship between mother and son was very poor. His elder and favourite sister Lady Leconfield was the wife of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Wyndham,_2nd_Baron_Leconfield" title="Henry Wyndham, 2nd Baron Leconfield">Henry Wyndham, 2nd Baron Leconfield</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education_and_youth">Education and youth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Education and youth">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Dalmeny attended preparatory schools in <a href="/wiki/Hertfordshire" title="Hertfordshire">Hertfordshire</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brighton" title="Brighton">Brighton</a>, and then <a href="/wiki/Eton_College" title="Eton College">Eton College</a> (1860–65<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>). At Eton, he formed a close attachment to his tutor <a href="/wiki/William_Johnson_Cory" title="William Johnson Cory">William Johnson Cory</a>: they visited <a href="/wiki/Rome" title="Rome">Rome</a> together in 1864, and maintained correspondence for years afterwards.<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Dalmeny proceeded to <a href="/wiki/Christ_Church,_Oxford" title="Christ Church, Oxford">Christ Church, Oxford</a>, matriculating in January 1866.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> He left Oxford in 1868:<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Dalmeny bought a horse named Ladas, although a rule banned <a href="/wiki/Undergraduates" class="mw-redirect" title="Undergraduates">undergraduates</a> from owning horses. When he was found out, he was offered a choice: to sell the horse or to give up his studies. He chose the latter, and subsequently was a prominent figure in British horseracing for 40 years.
</p><p>The three Prime Ministers from 1880 to 1902, namely <a href="/wiki/William_Ewart_Gladstone" title="William Ewart Gladstone">Gladstone</a>, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Arthur_Talbot_Gascoyne-Cecil,_3rd_Marquess_of_Salisbury" class="mw-redirect" title="Robert Arthur Talbot Gascoyne-Cecil, 3rd Marquess of Salisbury">Salisbury</a> and Rosebery, all attended both Eton and Christ Church. Rosebery toured the United States in 1873, 1874 and 1876. He was pressed to marry <a href="/wiki/Marie_Fox" title="Marie Fox">Marie Fox</a>, the sixteen-year-old adopted daughter of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Fox,_4th_Baron_Holland" title="Henry Fox, 4th Baron Holland">Henry Fox, 4th Baron Holland</a>. She declined him.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="Where is this prospective marriage mentioned? (May 2023)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Succession_to_earldom">Succession to earldom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Succession to earldom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output <p>When his grandfather died in 1868, Dalmeny became 5th <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Earl of Rosebery">Earl of Rosebery</a>. The earldom did not of itself entitle Archibald Primrose to sit in the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Lords" title="House of Lords">House of Lords</a>, nor disqualify him from sitting in the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Commons_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="House of Commons of the United Kingdom">House of Commons</a>. The title is part of the old <a href="/wiki/Peerage_of_Scotland" title="Peerage of Scotland">Peerage of Scotland</a>, from which 16 members (<a href="/wiki/Representative_peer" title="Representative peer">representative peers</a>) were elected to sit in the Lords for each session of Parliament. However, in 1828, Rosebery's grandfather had been created 1st <a href="/wiki/Baron_Rosebery" class="mw-redirect" title="Baron Rosebery">Baron Rosebery</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Peerage_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Peerage of the United Kingdom">Peerage of the United Kingdom</a>, which did entitle Rosebery to sit in the Lords like all peers of the United Kingdom, and barred him from a career in the House of Commons.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (November 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Career">Career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Rosebery is reputed to have said that he had three aims in life: to win <a href="/wiki/Epsom_Derby" title="Epsom Derby">the Derby</a>, to marry an heiress, and to become <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Prime Minister of the United Kingdom">Prime Minister</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> He managed all three.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_political_career">Early political career</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Early political career">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>At Eton, Rosebery notably attacked <a href="/wiki/Charles_I_of_England" title="Charles I of England">Charles I of England</a> for his <a href="/wiki/Despotism" title="Despotism">despotism</a>, and went on to praise his <a href="/wiki/Whigs_(British_political_party)" title="Whigs (British political party)">Whig</a> forebears – his ancestor, <a href="/wiki/James_Stanhope,_1st_Earl_Stanhope" title="James Stanhope, 1st Earl Stanhope">James Stanhope, 1st Earl Stanhope</a>, was a minister to <a href="/wiki/George_I_of_Great_Britain" title="George I of Great Britain">George I of Great Britain</a>. <a href="/wiki/Benjamin_Disraeli" title="Benjamin Disraeli">Benjamin Disraeli</a> often met with Rosebery in the 1870s to try to recruit him for his party, but this proved futile. Disraeli's major rival, <a href="/wiki/William_Ewart_Gladstone" title="William Ewart Gladstone">William Ewart Gladstone</a>, also pursued Rosebery, with considerable success.
</p><p>As part of the Liberal plan to get Gladstone to be MP for <a href="/wiki/Midlothian_(UK_Parliament_constituency)_(1708%E2%80%931918)" title="Midlothian (UK Parliament constituency) (1708–1918)">Midlothian</a>, Rosebery sponsored and largely ran the <a href="/wiki/Midlothian_Campaign" class="mw-redirect" title="Midlothian Campaign">Midlothian Campaign</a> of 1879. He based this on what he had observed in <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_the_United_States" title="Elections in the United States">elections in the United States</a>. Gladstone spoke from open-deck trains, and gathered mass support. In 1880, he was duly elected Member for Midlothian and returned to the premiership.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery served as Foreign Secretary in Gladstone's brief third ministry in 1886. He served as the first chairman of the London County Council, set up by the Conservatives in 1889. Rosebery Avenue in Clerkenwell is named after him.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> He served as <a href="/wiki/List_of_Presidents_of_Co-operative_Congress" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Presidents of Co-operative Congress">President</a> of the first day of the 1890 <a href="/wiki/Co-operative_Congress" title="Co-operative Congress">Co-operative Congress</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery's second period as Foreign Secretary, 1892–1894, predominantly involved quarrels with France over <a href="/wiki/Uganda" title="Uganda">Uganda</a>. To quote his hero <a href="/wiki/Napoleon" title="Napoleon">Napoleon</a>, Rosebery thought that "the Master of Egypt is the Master of India"; thus he pursued the policy of expansion in Africa. He helped Gladstone's Second Home Rule Bill in the House of Lords; nevertheless it was defeated overwhelmingly in the autumn of 1893.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The first bill had been defeated in the House of Commons in 1886.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_Minister">Prime Minister<span class="anchor" id="Premiership"></span></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Prime Minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Rosebery_ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="Rosebery ministry">Rosebery ministry</a></div>
<p>Rosebery became a leader of the Liberal Imperialist faction of the Liberal Party and when Gladstone retired, in 1894, Rosebery succeeded him as Prime Minister, much to the disgust of Sir <a href="/wiki/William_Vernon_Harcourt_(politician)" class="mw-redirect" title="William Vernon Harcourt (politician)">William Harcourt</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> and leader of the more left-wing Liberals. Rosebery's selection was largely because <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> disliked most of the other leading Liberals. Rosebery was in the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Lords" title="House of Lords">Lords</a>, but Harcourt controlled the <a href="/wiki/House_of_commons" class="mw-redirect" title="House of commons">Commons</a>, where he often undercut the prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery's government was largely unsuccessful, as in the <a href="/wiki/Hamidian_massacres" title="Hamidian massacres">Armenian crisis of 1895–96</a>. He spoke out for a strongly pro-Armenian and <a href="/wiki/Anti-Turkish" class="mw-redirect" title="Anti-Turkish">anti-Turkish</a> policy.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Gladstone, a prime minister in retirement, called on Britain to intervene alone. The added pressure weakened Rosebery.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>His designs in foreign policy, such as an expansion of the fleet, were defeated by disagreements within the Liberal Party. He angered all the European powers.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Unionist-dominated House of Lords stopped the whole of the Liberals' domestic legislation. The strongest figure in the cabinet was Rosebery's rival, Harcourt. He and his son <a href="/wiki/Lewis_Harcourt" class="mw-redirect" title="Lewis Harcourt">Lewis</a> were perennial critics of Rosebery's policies. There were two future prime ministers in the Cabinet, <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Home_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary of State for the Home Department">Home Secretary</a> <a href="/wiki/H._H._Asquith" title="H. H. Asquith">H. H. Asquith</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_War" title="Secretary of State for War">Secretary of State for War</a> <a href="/wiki/Henry_Campbell-Bannerman" title="Henry Campbell-Bannerman">Henry Campbell-Bannerman</a>. Rosebery rapidly lost interest in running the government. In the last year of his premiership, he was increasingly haggard: he suffered <a href="/wiki/Insomnia" title="Insomnia">insomnia</a> due to the continual dissension in his Cabinet.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 21 June 1895, the government <a href="/wiki/1895_vote_of_no_confidence_in_the_Rosebery_ministry" title="1895 vote of no confidence in the Rosebery ministry">lost a vote</a> in committee on army supply by just seven votes. While this might have been treated merely as a vote of no confidence in Secretary for War Campbell-Bannerman, Rosebery chose to treat it as a vote of censure on his government. On 22 June, he and his ministers tendered their resignations to the Queen, who invited the Unionist leader, <a href="/wiki/Robert_Gascoyne-Cecil,_3rd_Marquess_of_Salisbury" title="Robert Gascoyne-Cecil, 3rd Marquess of Salisbury">Lord Salisbury</a>, to form a government. The following month, the Unionists won a crushing victory in the <a href="/wiki/1895_United_Kingdom_general_election" title="1895 United Kingdom general election">1895 general election</a>, and held power for ten years (1895–1905) under Salisbury and <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Balfour" title="Arthur Balfour">Arthur Balfour</a>. Rosebery remained the Liberal leader for another year, then permanently retired from politics.
</p>
<h4><span id="Lord_Rosebery.27s_government.2C_March_1894_.E2.80.93_June_1895"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Lord_Rosebery's_government,_March_1894_–_June_1895">Lord Rosebery's government, March 1894 – June 1895</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Lord Rosebery&#039;s government, March 1894 – June 1895">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li>Lord Rosebery – <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Treasury" title="First Lord of the Treasury">First Lord of the Treasury</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lord_President_of_the_Council" title="Lord President of the Council">Lord President of the Council</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_of_Lords" title="Leader of the House of Lords">Leader of the House of Lords</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Farrer_Herschell,_1st_Baron_Herschell" title="Farrer Herschell, 1st Baron Herschell">Lord Herschell</a> – <a href="/wiki/Lord_Chancellor" title="Lord Chancellor">Lord Chancellor</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Edward_Marjoribanks,_2nd_Baron_Tweedmouth" title="Edward Marjoribanks, 2nd Baron Tweedmouth">Lord Tweedmouth</a> – <a href="/wiki/Lord_Privy_Seal" title="Lord Privy Seal">Lord Privy Seal</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/H._H._Asquith" title="H. H. Asquith">H. H. Asquith</a> – <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_the_Home_Department" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary of State for the Home Department">Secretary of State for the Home Department</a></li>

<li><a href="/wiki/Henry_Hartley_Fowler,_1st_Viscount_Wolverhampton" class="mw-redirect" title="Henry Hartley Fowler, 1st Viscount Wolverhampton">Sir Henry Hartley Fowler</a> – <a href="/wiki/Secretary_of_State_for_India" title="Secretary of State for India">Secretary of State for India</a></li>
<li>Sir <a href="/wiki/William_Vernon_Harcourt_(politician)" class="mw-redirect" title="William Vernon Harcourt (politician)">William Harcourt</a> – <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Exchequer" title="Chancellor of the Exchequer">Chancellor of the Exchequer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_House_of_Commons" title="Leader of the House of Commons">Leader of the House of Commons</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Poyntz_Spencer,_5th_Earl_Spencer" class="mw-redirect" title="John Poyntz Spencer, 5th Earl Spencer">Lord Spencer</a> – <a href="/wiki/First_Lord_of_the_Admiralty" title="First Lord of the Admiralty">First Lord of the Admiralty</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Anthony_John_Mundella" class="mw-redirect" title="Anthony John Mundella">Anthony John Mundella</a> – <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Board_of_Trade" title="President of the Board of Trade">President of the Board of Trade</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Arnold_Morley" title="Arnold Morley">Arnold Morley</a> – <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom_Postmaster_General" class="mw-redirect" title="United Kingdom Postmaster General">Postmaster-General</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/George_John_Shaw-Lefevre,_1st_Baron_Eversley" class="mw-redirect" title="George John Shaw-Lefevre, 1st Baron Eversley">George John Shaw-Lefevre</a> – <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Local_Government_Board" title="President of the Local Government Board">President of the Local Government Board</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/James_Bryce,_1st_Viscount_Bryce" title="James Bryce, 1st Viscount Bryce">James Bryce</a> – <a href="/wiki/Chancellor_of_the_Duchy_of_Lancaster" title="Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster">Chancellor of the Duchy of Lancaster</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/John_Morley" title="John Morley">John Morley</a> – <a href="/wiki/Chief_Secretary_for_Ireland" title="Chief Secretary for Ireland">Chief Secretary for Ireland</a></li>
<li>Sir <a href="/wiki/George_Otto_Trevelyan" class="mw-redirect" title="George Otto Trevelyan">George Otto Trevelyan</a> – <a href="/wiki/Secretary_for_Scotland" class="mw-redirect" title="Secretary for Scotland">Secretary for Scotland</a></li>
<li>Sir <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Herbert_Dyke_Acland" class="mw-redirect" title="Arthur Herbert Dyke Acland">Arthur Herbert Dyke Acland</a> – <a href="/wiki/Vice-President_of_the_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Vice-President of the Council">Vice-President of the Council</a></li></ul>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Changes">Changes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Changes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li>May 1894: James Bryce succeeds A. J. Mundella at the Board of Trade. Lord Tweedmouth succeeds Bryce at the Duchy of Lancaster, remaining also Lord Privy Seal.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Later_life">Later life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Later life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg/220px-Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg/330px-Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg/440px-Earl_of_Rosebery_Vanity_Fair_14_March_1901.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2132" data-file-height="3200" /></a><figcaption>Rosebery caricatured by <a href="/wiki/Leslie_Ward" title="Leslie Ward">Spy</a> for <a href="/wiki/Vanity_Fair_(British_magazine)" title="Vanity Fair (British magazine)"><i>Vanity Fair</i></a>, 1901</figcaption></figure>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Liberal_Imperialists">Liberal Imperialists</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Liberal Imperialists">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Rosebery resigned as leader of the Liberal Party on 6 October 1896, to be succeeded by William Harcourt and gradually moved further and further from the mainstream of the party.  With the Liberals in opposition divided over the <a href="/wiki/Second_Boer_War" title="Second Boer War">Boer War</a> which started in 1899, Rosebery, although officially politically inactive, emerged as the head of the “<a href="/wiki/Liberal_Imperialists" title="Liberal Imperialists">Liberal Imperialists</a>” faction of the party, opposed to Irish <a href="/wiki/Home_rule" title="Home rule">Home rule</a>. He supported the war, and brought along many nonconformists likewise.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> However the war was opposed by a younger faction of Liberals, including <a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George" title="David Lloyd George">David Lloyd George</a> and the party leader <a href="/wiki/Sir_Henry_Campbell-Bannerman" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Henry Campbell-Bannerman">Sir Henry Campbell-Bannerman</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Rosebery's acolytes, including <a href="/wiki/H._H._Asquith" title="H. H. Asquith">H. H. Asquith</a> and <a href="/wiki/Edward_Grey,_1st_Viscount_Grey_of_Fallodon" title="Edward Grey, 1st Viscount Grey of Fallodon">Edward Grey</a>, regularly implored him to return as party leader and even Campbell-Bannerman said he would serve under Rosebery, if he accepted fundamental Liberal party doctrine.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> In a much trailed speech to the Chesterfield Liberal Association in December 1901, Rosebery was widely expected to announce his return but instead delivered what Harcourt's son and private secretary <a href="/wiki/Lewis_Harcourt" class="mw-redirect" title="Lewis Harcourt">Lewis</a> described as "an insult to the whole past of the Liberal party", by telling the party to "clean its slate".<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> In 1902 Rosebery was installed as president of the newly formed “<a href="/wiki/Liberal_League_(United_Kingdom)" title="Liberal League (United Kingdom)">Liberal League</a>” which superseded the Liberal Imperialist League and counted amongst its vice presidents Asquith and Grey.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He was <a href="/wiki/Colonel_(United_Kingdom)#Honorary_Colonel" title="Colonel (United Kingdom)">Honorary Colonel</a> of the <a href="/wiki/1st_Midlothian_Artillery_Volunteers" title="1st Midlothian Artillery Volunteers">1st Midlothian Artillery Volunteers</a> from January 1903 until his death in 1929.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="1905_onwards">1905 onwards</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: 1905 onwards">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Rosebery's positions made it impossible to join the Liberal government that returned to power in 1905. Rosebery turned to writing, including biographies of <a href="/wiki/William_Pitt_the_Elder" class="mw-redirect" title="William Pitt the Elder">Lord Chatham</a>, <a href="/wiki/William_Pitt_the_Younger" title="William Pitt the Younger">Pitt the Younger</a>, <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_I_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Napoleon I of France">Napoleon</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lord_Randolph_Churchill" title="Lord Randolph Churchill">Lord Randolph Churchill</a>. Another one of his passionate interests was the collecting of rare books.
</p><p>The last years of his political life saw Rosebery become a purely negative critic of the Liberal governments of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Campbell-Bannerman" title="Henry Campbell-Bannerman">Campbell-Bannerman</a> and <a href="/wiki/H._H._Asquith" title="H. H. Asquith">Asquith</a>. His crusade "for freedom as against bureaucracy, for freedom as against democratic tyranny, for freedom as against class legislation, and ... for freedom as against Socialism"<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> was a lonely one, conducted from the crossbenches in the Lords. He joined the die-hard unionist peers in attacking <a href="/wiki/David_Lloyd_George" title="David Lloyd George">Lloyd George</a>'s redistributive <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Budget" title="People&#39;s Budget">People's Budget</a> in 1909 but stopped short of voting against the measure for fear of bringing retribution upon the Lords. The crisis provoked by the Lords' rejection of the budget encouraged him to reintroduce his resolutions for Lords reform, but they were lost with the dissolution of parliament in December 1910.
</p><p>After assaulting the "ill-judged, revolutionary and partisan" terms of the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_Act_1911" title="Parliament Act 1911">1911 Parliament Bill</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> which proposed to curb the Lords' veto, he voted with the government in what proved to be his last appearance in the House of Lords. This was effectively the end of his public life, though he made several public appearances to support the <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">war</a> effort after 1914 and sponsored a "<a href="/wiki/Bantam_battalion" class="mw-redirect" title="Bantam battalion">bantam battalion</a>" in 1915. Though Lloyd George offered him "a high post not involving departmental labour" to augment his 1916 coalition, Rosebery declined to serve.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Personal_life">Personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Marriage">Marriage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Marriage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-More_citations_needed_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Special:EditPage/Archibald Primrose, 5th Earl of Rosebery">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>&#32;in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">March 2016</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:HdeR.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ab/HdeR.png/170px-HdeR.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="279" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ab/HdeR.png/255px-HdeR.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/a/ab/HdeR.png/340px-HdeR.png 2x" data-file-width="423" data-file-height="695" /></a><figcaption>Hannah de Rothschild, portrait by <a href="/wiki/Frederic_Leighton,_1st_Baron_Leighton" class="mw-redirect" title="Frederic Leighton, 1st Baron Leighton">Frederic Leighton, 1st Baron Leighton</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>On 20 March 1878, 31-year-old Rosebery married 27-year-old <a href="/wiki/Hannah_Primrose,_Countess_of_Rosebery" title="Hannah Primrose, Countess of Rosebery">Hannah de Rothschild</a> (1851–1890), only child and sole heiress of the Jewish banker <a href="/wiki/Mayer_Amschel_de_Rothschild" title="Mayer Amschel de Rothschild">Mayer Amschel de Rothschild</a>, and the wealthiest British heiress of her day. Her father had died four years previously in 1874, and bequeathed to her the bulk of his estate. The wedding was held (registered) at the office of the <a href="/wiki/Board_of_Guardians" class="mw-redirect" title="Board of Guardians">Board of Guardians</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mount_Street,_London" title="Mount Street, London">Mount Street, London</a>. Later the same day, the marriage was blessed at a Christian ceremony in Christ Church, Down Street, <a href="/wiki/Piccadilly" title="Piccadilly">Piccadilly</a>. <a href="/wiki/Edward_VII_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Edward VII of the United Kingdom">The Prince of Wales</a> and the Queen's cousin, the army commander <a href="/wiki/Prince_George,_Duke_of_Cambridge" title="Prince George, Duke of Cambridge">Prince George, Duke of Cambridge</a>, were among the guests who attended the ceremony.
</p><p>The marriage was a happy one. In January 1878, Rosebery had told a friend that he found Hannah "very simple, very unspoilt, very clever, very warm-hearted and very shy ... I never knew such a beautiful character." Hannah's death in 1890 from <a href="/wiki/Typhoid" class="mw-redirect" title="Typhoid">typhoid</a>, compounded by <a href="/wiki/Bright%27s_disease" title="Bright&#39;s disease">Bright's disease</a>, left him distraught.
</p><p>More than a decade after his wife's death, in July 1901, it was speculated that Rosebery intended to marry the widowed <a href="/wiki/Princess_Helena,_Duchess_of_Albany" class="mw-redirect" title="Princess Helena, Duchess of Albany">Princess Helena, Duchess of Albany</a>, widow of <a href="/wiki/Prince_Leopold,_Duke_of_Albany" title="Prince Leopold, Duke of Albany">Prince Leopold, Duke of Albany</a>, youngest son of <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Princess Helena was also the sister of <a href="/wiki/Emma_of_Waldeck_and_Pyrmont" title="Emma of Waldeck and Pyrmont">Queen Emma of the Netherlands</a>. However, Rosebery never remarried.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Progeny">Progeny</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Progeny">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>By his wife Hannah de Rothschild, Rosebery had two sons and two daughters, with whom, according to <a href="/wiki/Margot_Asquith" title="Margot Asquith">Margot Asquith</a>, he loved to play:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Harry_Primrose,_6th_Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Harry Primrose, 6th Earl of Rosebery">Albert Edward Harry Meyer Archibald Primrose, 6th Earl of Rosebery</a> known as <i>Harry</i> (8 January 1882 – 30 May 1974) he married Lady Dorothy Grosvenor (granddaughter of <a href="/wiki/Hugh_Lupus_Grosvenor,_1st_Duke_of_Westminster" class="mw-redirect" title="Hugh Lupus Grosvenor, 1st Duke of Westminster">Hugh Lupus Grosvenor, 1st Duke of Westminster</a> through his third son Lord Henry Grosvenor) on 15 April 1909 and was divorced from her in 1919. They had two children. He married Hon. Eva Isabel Bruce (daughter of <a href="/wiki/Henry_Bruce,_2nd_Baron_Aberdare" title="Henry Bruce, 2nd Baron Aberdare">Henry Campbell Bruce, 2nd Baron Aberdare</a>) on 24 June 1924. They had two children.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Neil_James_Archibald_Primrose" class="mw-redirect" title="Neil James Archibald Primrose">Neil James Archibald Primrose</a> (14 December 1882 – 18 November 1917) he married Lady Victoria Stanley (daughter <a href="/wiki/Edward_Stanley,_17th_Earl_of_Derby" title="Edward Stanley, 17th Earl of Derby">Edward Stanley, 17th Earl of Derby</a>) on 7 April 1915. They had one daughter: <a href="/wiki/Ruth_Wood,_Countess_of_Halifax" title="Ruth Wood, Countess of Halifax">Ruth Wood, Countess of Halifax</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Sybil_Grant" class="mw-redirect" title="Sybil Grant">Lady Sybil Primrose</a> (1879–25 February 1955) she married General Sir <a href="/wiki/Charles_Grant_(British_Army_officer)" title="Charles Grant (British Army officer)">Charles Grant</a> on 28 March 1903. They had one son.</li>
<li>Lady Margaret "Peggy" Etrenne Hannah Primrose<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> (1 January 1881 – 13 March 1967) she married <a href="/wiki/Robert_Crewe-Milnes,_1st_Marquess_of_Crewe" title="Robert Crewe-Milnes, 1st Marquess of Crewe">Robert Crewe-Milnes, 1st Marquess of Crewe</a> on 20 April 1899. They had two children. As Lady Crewe, she became one of the first seven women appointed as magistrates in 1919 following the passing of the <a href="/wiki/Sex_Disqualification_(Removal)_Act_1919" title="Sex Disqualification (Removal) Act 1919">Sex Disqualification (Removal) Act 1919</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sexuality">Sexuality</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Sexuality">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Throughout his life, it was rumoured that Rosebery was <a href="/wiki/Homosexual" class="mw-redirect" title="Homosexual">homosexual</a> or <a href="/wiki/Bisexual" class="mw-redirect" title="Bisexual">bisexual</a>. He was a notorious <a href="/wiki/Misogynist" class="mw-redirect" title="Misogynist">misogynist</a>, and liked to surround himself with younger men.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a student at Eton, beyond his close relationship with his tutor, <a href="/wiki/William_Johnson_Cory" title="William Johnson Cory">William Johnson Cory</a>, he likely had feelings for at least one fellow student, Frederick Vyner. He was devastated by his <a href="/wiki/Dilessi_murders" title="Dilessi murders">murder at the hands of Greek brigands in 1870</a>, keeping the anniversary sacred for the rest of his life.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Like <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Wilde" title="Oscar Wilde">Oscar Wilde</a>, he was hounded by <a href="/wiki/John_Douglas,_9th_Marquess_of_Queensberry" title="John Douglas, 9th Marquess of Queensberry">John Douglas, 9th Marquess of Queensberry</a> for his association with <a href="/wiki/Francis_Douglas,_Viscount_Drumlanrig" title="Francis Douglas, Viscount Drumlanrig">Francis Douglas, Viscount Drumlanrig</a>, Queensberry's first born son<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> – who had become his private secretary in 1892 when Rosebery became Foreign Secretary. A few months later he arranged for Drumlanrig, who was 26 at the time, to be made a junior member of the government with a seat in the House of Lords.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the preliminary hearing of the case against Wilde, a letter from Queensberry was produced referring to him as 'a damned cur and coward of the Rosebery type'.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 18 October 1894, sixteen months after his ennoblement, Drumlanrig died from injuries received during a shooting party. The inquest returned a verdict of "accidental death", but his death was rumoured potentially to be suicide or murder.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> It was speculated at the time<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> that Drumlanrig may have had a romantic, if not sexual, relationship with Rosebery.
</p><p>The suggestion was that Queensberry had threatened to expose the Prime Minister if his government did not vigorously prosecute Wilde for Wilde's relationship with Drumlanrig's younger brother, <a href="/wiki/Lord_Alfred_Douglas" title="Lord Alfred Douglas">Lord Alfred Douglas</a>. Queensberry believed, as he put it in a letter, that "Snob Queers like Rosebery" had corrupted his sons, and he held Rosebery indirectly responsible for Drumlanrig's death.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> He claimed to have evidence of Rosebery's transgressions but that was never confirmed.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Using a minor defeat in Parliament that did not warrant such action, Rosebery resigned from the Premiership on 22 June 1895. This was a few months after the death of Drumlanrig and not quite a month after Wilde was convicted on 25 May, his life and reputation destroyed by a man who was also pursuing Rosebery for the same reason he was after Wilde. In August 1893, Queensberry had followed Rosebery to the spa town of <a href="/wiki/Bad_Homburg" title="Bad Homburg">Bad Homburg</a> with the declared intention of giving him a horse-whipping, and had to be dissuaded by the Prince of Wales who was also staying there.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In his recollections, Rosebery wrote: "I cannot forget 1895. To lie awake night after night, wide awake, hopeless of sleep, tormented of nerves, and to realise all that was going on, at which I was present, so to speak, like a disembodied spirit, to watch one's own corpse, as it were day after day, is an experience which no sane man would repeat."<sup id="cite_ref-:1_45-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Sir_Edmund_Backhouse,_2nd_Baronet" title="Sir Edmund Backhouse, 2nd Baronet">Sir Edmund Backhouse</a> wrote in his unpublished memoirs that he had been one of Rosebery's lovers - although it has been suggested that many of Backhouse's claims were dubiously made.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Robert_Rhodes_James" title="Robert Rhodes James">Robert Rhodes James</a>, who wrote a biography of Rosebery in 1963 (when homosexuality was still illegal in Britain), makes no mention of homosexual relationships at all, while for <a href="/wiki/Leo_McKinstry" title="Leo McKinstry">Leo McKinstry</a>, who was writing in 2005, the evidence that Rosebery was homosexual is circumstantial. <a href="/wiki/Michael_Bloch" title="Michael Bloch">Michael Bloch</a>, in 2015, has, however, no doubt that Rosebery was at least romantically interested in men, making him one of the four figures presented in the first chapter of his book on homosexual and bisexual British politicians of the 20th century. In his view, any remaining evidence (of which he gives a long list) can only be circumstantial in any case, considering Rosebery's paranoid taste for secrecy.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death_and_burial">Death and burial</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Death and burial">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Durdans_(1)_(geograph_2742620).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg/220px-Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg/330px-Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg/440px-Durdans_%281%29_%28geograph_2742620%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="424" /></a><figcaption>Durdans, Woodcote End, Epsom, Surrey, England was the place of Rosebery's demise in 1929, shown in 2011. Its gardens are smaller than when engraved by <a href="/wiki/John_Hassell_(artist)" class="mw-redirect" title="John Hassell (artist)">John Hassell</a> in 1816.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The last year of the war was clouded by two personal tragedies: his son <a href="/wiki/Neil_Primrose_(politician)" title="Neil Primrose (politician)">Neil</a>'s death in Palestine in November 1917 and Rosebery's own <a href="/wiki/Stroke" title="Stroke">stroke</a> a few days before the <a href="/wiki/Armistice_with_Germany_(Compi%C3%A8gne)" class="mw-redirect" title="Armistice with Germany (Compiègne)">armistice</a>. He regained his mental powers, but his movement, hearing, and sight remained impaired for the rest of his life. His sister Constance described his last years as a "life of weariness, of total inactivity, and at the last of almost blindness". <a href="/wiki/John_Buchan" title="John Buchan">John Buchan</a> remembered him in his last month of life, "crushed by bodily weakness" and "sunk in sad and silent meditations".<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Rosebery died at his <a href="/wiki/Epsom" title="Epsom">Epsom</a>, Surrey home of The Durdans on 21 May 1929, to the accompaniment, as he had requested, of a gramophone recording of the "<a href="/wiki/Eton_Boating_Song" title="Eton Boating Song">Eton Boating Song</a>". Survived by three of his four children, he was buried in the small church at <a href="/wiki/Dalmeny" title="Dalmeny">Dalmeny</a>. By the time of his death, he was the last <a href="/wiki/Victorian-era" class="mw-redirect" title="Victorian-era">Victorian-era</a> British Prime Minister alive.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sporting_interests">Sporting interests</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Sporting interests">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">March 2016</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Horse_racing">Horse racing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Horse racing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As a result of his marriage to Hannah de Rothschild, Rosebery acquired the <a href="/wiki/Mentmore_Towers#The_Rosebery_era" title="Mentmore Towers">Mentmore Towers</a> estate and Mentmore <a href="/wiki/Stud_farm" title="Stud farm">stud</a> near <a href="/wiki/Leighton_Buzzard" title="Leighton Buzzard">Leighton Buzzard</a> which had been built by <a href="/wiki/Mayer_Amschel_de_Rothschild" title="Mayer Amschel de Rothschild">Mayer Amschel de Rothschild</a>. Rosebery built another stable and stud near Mentmore Towers at <a href="/wiki/Crafton,_Buckinghamshire" title="Crafton, Buckinghamshire">Crafton, Buckinghamshire</a>, called <a href="/wiki/Crafton_Stud" class="mw-redirect" title="Crafton Stud">Crafton Stud</a>.
</p><p>Rosebery won several of the five <a href="/wiki/British_Classic_Races" title="British Classic Races">English Classic Races</a>. His most famous horses were <a href="/wiki/Ladas_(horse)" title="Ladas (horse)">Ladas</a> who won the 1894 <a href="/wiki/Epsom_Derby" title="Epsom Derby">Derby</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sir_Visto" title="Sir Visto">Sir Visto</a> who did it again in 1895 (Rosebery was Prime Minister on both occasions), and <a href="/wiki/Cicero_(horse)" title="Cicero (horse)">Cicero</a> in 1905.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Football">Football</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Football">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-Unreferenced_section plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Unreferenced" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>does not <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citing_sources" title="Wikipedia:Citing sources">cite</a> any <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">sources</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit">improve this section</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Burden_of_evidence" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">removed</a>.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">March 2016</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Rosebery became the first president of the <a href="/wiki/London_Scottish_F.C." title="London Scottish F.C.">London Scottish Rugby Football Club</a> in 1878, also developed a keen interest in association football and was an early patron of the sport in Scotland. In 1882 he donated a trophy, the <a href="/wiki/Rosebery_Charity_Cup" title="Rosebery Charity Cup">Rosebery Charity Cup</a>, to be competed for by clubs under the jurisdiction of the East of Scotland Football Association. The competition lasted over sixty years and raised thousands of pounds for charities in the <a href="/wiki/Edinburgh" title="Edinburgh">Edinburgh</a> area.
</p><p>Rosebery also became Honorary President of the national <a href="/wiki/Scottish_Football_Association" title="Scottish Football Association">Scottish Football Association</a>, with the representative <a href="/wiki/Scotland_national_football_team" title="Scotland national football team">Scotland national team</a> and Honorary President of <a href="/wiki/Heart_of_Midlothian_F.C." title="Heart of Midlothian F.C.">Heart of Midlothian</a>. The national team occasionally forsook their traditional dark blue shirts for his traditional racing colours of primrose and pink. This occurred nine times during Rosebery's lifetime, most notably for the 1900 <a href="/wiki/British_Home_Championship" title="British Home Championship">British Home Championship</a> match against <a href="/wiki/England_national_football_team" title="England national football team">England</a>, which the Scots won 4–1. These colours were used for the away kit of the Scotland national team in 2014<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> and were Heart of Midlothian's away colours for season 2016/17.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Literary_interests">Literary interests</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Literary interests">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>He was a keen collector of fine books and amassed an excellent library. It was sold on 29 October 2009 at Sothebys, New Bond Street. Rosebery unveiled the statue of <a href="/wiki/Robert_Burns" title="Robert Burns">Robert Burns</a> in <a href="/wiki/Dumfries" title="Dumfries">Dumfries</a> on 6 April 1882.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Landholdings">Landholdings</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Landholdings">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg/220px-Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg/330px-Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/23/Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg/440px-Dalmenyhouse_lighter.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Dalmeny_House" title="Dalmeny House">Dalmeny House</a> was the ancestral seat of the <a href="/wiki/Earl_of_Rosebery" title="Earl of Rosebery">Earls of Rosebery</a> and the setting for Lord and Lady Rosebery's political houseparties.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg/220px-Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg/330px-Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e9/Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg/440px-Mentmore_Towers_from_angle.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="328" /></a><figcaption>Mentmore Towers</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg/220px-Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg/330px-Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/be/Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg/440px-Napoli_-_Villa_Rosebery2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3456" data-file-height="2304" /></a><figcaption>Villa Delahente now Villa Rosebery</figcaption></figure>
<p>Rosebery was the owner of twelve houses. By marriage, he acquired:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Mentmore_Towers" title="Mentmore Towers">Mentmore Towers</a> in Buckinghamshire, a huge neo-Renaissance stately home, sold in the 1970s</li>
<li>Number 40, <a href="/wiki/Piccadilly" title="Piccadilly">Piccadilly</a>, in London.</li></ul>
<p>With his fortune, he bought:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Malleny_House_and_Garden" title="Malleny House and Garden">Malleny House and Garden</a> in 1882<sup id="cite_ref-MallenyGarden_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MallenyGarden-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>a shooting lodge at <a href="/wiki/Carrington,_Midlothian" title="Carrington, Midlothian">Carrington</a> in <a href="/wiki/Midlothian" title="Midlothian">Midlothian</a></li>
<li>a Georgian villa at <a href="/wiki/Postwick" class="mw-redirect" title="Postwick">Postwick</a> in <a href="/wiki/Norfolk" title="Norfolk">Norfolk</a></li>
<li>In 1897, he bought Villa Delahente in <a href="/wiki/Posillipo" title="Posillipo">Posillipo</a>, overlooking the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Naples" class="mw-redirect" title="Bay of Naples">Bay of Naples</a>, currently an official residence of the President of the Italian Republic, still known as <a href="/wiki/Villa_Rosebery" title="Villa Rosebery">Villa Rosebery</a></li>
<li>38 Berkeley Square, London</li>
<li>The Durdans, <a href="/wiki/Epsom" title="Epsom">Epsom</a>, where he died in 1929.</li></ul>
<p>As Earl of Rosebery, he was laird of:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Dalmeny_House" title="Dalmeny House">Dalmeny House</a> on the banks of the <a href="/wiki/Firth_of_Forth" title="Firth of Forth">Firth of Forth</a> (pictured)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Barnbougle_Castle" title="Barnbougle Castle">Barnbougle Castle</a> in the grounds of Dalmeny Estate, used by Rosebery (an insomniac) for privacy.</li></ul>
<p>He rented:
</p>
<ul><li>a home in Randolph Crescent, <a href="/wiki/Edinburgh" title="Edinburgh">Edinburgh</a>, during <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Lansdowne_House" title="Lansdowne House">Lansdowne House</a>, in London, from the Marquess of Lansdowne.</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy_and_evaluations">Legacy and evaluations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Legacy and evaluations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Rosebery's position in British politics was puzzling to contemporaries and historians due to the enigmatic nature of his private and public lives. He had an air of privileged detachment, which persisted throughout his brief stint in the political limelight and his significant years in the background. Although he was an orator and statesman in the mold of his original leader, Gladstone, his fifteen-month term as Liberal Prime Minister in 1894-5 was an unhappy spectacle. Lord Rosebery's failure to live up to his potential disappointed Liberals of all kinds. Journalists and biographers have criticized his lack of character and sense of failure, possibly influenced by his Scottish <a href="/wiki/Calvinist" class="mw-redirect" title="Calvinist">Calvinist</a> upbringing. Despite his love for luxury and pleasure, his motives for leaving and returning to politics may not have been solely self-indulgent. He was known for his passion for racehorses, even ending his studies at Oxford to pursue them.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Place-name_tributes">Place-name tributes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Place-name tributes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <i><a href="/wiki/Oatlands,_Glasgow" title="Oatlands, Glasgow">Oatlands</a></i> area in the South Side of <a href="/wiki/Glasgow" title="Glasgow">Glasgow</a> was laid out in the late 19th and early 20th centuries, contemporary with Rosebery's most prominent period. The area is much changed since it was originally laid out, but several of the original street names had an association with him or areas around his estate to the northwest of Edinburgh: Rosebery Street, Dalmeny Street, Queensferry Street, Granton Street and Cramond Street.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In London, <a href="/wiki/Rosebery_Avenue" title="Rosebery Avenue">Rosebery Avenue</a>, running between <a href="/wiki/Holborn" title="Holborn">Holborn</a> and <a href="/wiki/Clerkenwell" title="Clerkenwell">Clerkenwell</a>, was named after him, in recognition of his service as the <a href="/wiki/London_County_Council" title="London County Council">London County Council</a>'s first chairman.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Rosebery,_New_South_Wales" title="Rosebery, New South Wales">Rosebery, New South Wales</a>, a suburb of Sydney, is named after him. A major street, Dalmeny Avenue, runs through the area. <a href="/wiki/Rosebery,_Tasmania" title="Rosebery, Tasmania">Rosebery, Tasmania</a> is also named after him, via the name of a mining company. <a href="/wiki/Dalmeny,_New_South_Wales" title="Dalmeny, New South Wales">Dalmeny, New South Wales</a>, a suburb on the New South Wales <a href="/wiki/South_Coast_(New_South_Wales)" title="South Coast (New South Wales)">South Coast</a>, is named after him. Roseberry Avenue in the suburb of South Perth, <a href="/wiki/Western_Australia" title="Western Australia">Western Australia</a>, is also named after him. The former township of Rosebery in South Australia (now part of Collinswood) was named for him, as was modern-day Rosebery Lane in Collinswood.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Rosebery in the north west of Victoria, some 15&#160;km south of Hopetoun is also named after him.
</p><p>Rosebery House, <a href="/wiki/Epsom_College" title="Epsom College">Epsom College</a>, in Epsom, is named after him.
<a href="/wiki/Rosebery_School" class="mw-redirect" title="Rosebery School">Rosebery School</a> sits on an area of land given to the borough by Lord Rosebery.
</p><p>In October 1895 Lord Rosebery opened the new <i>Liberal Club</i> on Westborough, in <a href="/wiki/Scarborough,_North_Yorkshire" title="Scarborough, North Yorkshire">Scarborough</a>, only months after being <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister">Prime Minister</a>. The building now houses a <a href="/wiki/Wetherspoons" title="Wetherspoons">Wetherspoons</a>, which is named in his honour.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancestry">Ancestry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Archibald_Primrose,_5th_Earl_of_Rosebery&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Ancestry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
